Since tangents MA and MB are perpendicular, the angle between them is 90 degrees. \n\nFirst, we need to find the length of the tangents MA and MB. The length of a tangent from an external point to a circle is given by sqrt(d^2 - r^2), where d is the distance from the external point to the center and r is the radius of the circle. Here, r = 1. So, MA = MB = sqrt(d^2 - 1).\n\nSince MA and MB are perpendicular, the triangle AMB is a right triangle with right angle at M. The hypotenuse AB is the chord of the circle, and the length of AB can be found using the Pythagorean theorem: AB = sqrt(2*(sqrt(d^2 -1))^2) = sqrt(2)*(sqrt(d^2 -1)).\n\nHowever, the exact value of d isn\'t given.
